Nothing resolves "the" batch group by matching a student's `course_level`/`institution` anymore, on either the grant side or the removal side (see the `fn_auto_enroll_batch_group` retirement below). `create_batch_group` now only creates the group row (no bulk auto-add, pending or active). `enroll_user_in_batch_group` was resigned to take an explicit `p_group_id` (was: guessed from course+institution) and raises `'Not a batch group'` rather than silently doing nothing. `join_group_by_token` and `get_group_preview` are extended (return shape changed) to carry the new `'requested'` state; behavior for non-batch groups and non-student callers is unchanged. Batch membership never writes `profiles.course_level`/`institution` — those stay a separate, not-yet-built action. The batch invite/join screen (`GroupJoin.jsx`) discloses, for batch groups only, that joining shares the student's study activity and progress with that batch's institution — a custom (non-batch) group carries no such disclosure since it has no monitoring relationship.
- **Why:** real institutions run multiple batches per course level (e.g. May-attempt and Sept-attempt cohorts), so "the one batch group for this course+institution" was never a safe assumption — a course+institution match could legitimately resolve to the wrong one of several batches, on either the add or the remove side. A forwarded invite link is also not proof of being a genuine, paying student of that specific batch, and that risk applies to the founder's own class, not just future B2B clients. Approval was scoped to a plain admin-approves-individually queue for now (no delegated non-admin staff, no roster upload, no payment check) — deliberately smaller than a full institutional-staff workflow, which is real, separate future work. Disclosing the monitoring relationship on the join screen is informed consent — a student should know what joining actually grants the institution before requesting it, even though the batch/cohort itself stays out of the student's own Groups-page navigation (pre-existing, unrelated behavior).
- **Verified before building:** group-shared content (`get_browsable_notes`/`get_browsable_decks`/`get_group_detail`) already gates everywhere on `sgm.status = 'active'`; live RLS dump confirmed `notes`/`flashcards`/`flashcard_decks` base-table policies have no group-share clause at all (own/public/friends/admin only), so there is no direct-table path around that gate. A `'requested'` row is therefore already fully inert for content access — no paid/free content-tier concept was needed to make approval real.
- **Impact:** `study_group_members.status` CHECK now allows `'invited' | 'active' | 'requested'` (distinct from `'invited'`, which is the *opposite* direction — staff-initiated, awaiting the student's own accept). New RPCs `approve_batch_join_request`/`reject_batch_join_request`/`get_admin_pending_batch_requests`. `get_admin_batch_groups` gained `invite_token` (was never returned before) so AdminDashboard can show "Copy Invite Link" per batch. `create_batch_group` also gained an `is_admin()` guard it was missing entirely (any authenticated user could previously call it — same bug class as the already-fixed `enroll_user_in_batch_group`/`notify_access_granted`, security/15). **Follow-up (Quality Auditor review, same day):** `fn_auto_enroll_batch_group`'s course-change cleanup branch — removing membership from a student's *old* matched batch group — was found to carry the identical course+institution `LIMIT 1` ambiguity as the already-removed "add" branch, just on the removal side: a course change could silently drop a student from the wrong batch. Rather than patch the lookup, the trigger and function were retired entirely (`docs/database/sprint8.0/04_SCHEMA_retire_auto_enroll_trigger.sql`) — with both branches gone it did nothing, so nothing was lost by dropping it. Batch membership removal is now exclusively the pre-existing explicit `remove_group_member`/`leave_group` path, never automatic.
- **Explicitly out of scope, noted for later (Quality Auditor review):** no automated "inactive student" nudge/engagement feature was built or implied by this sprint. Whenever one is designed, it must not equate "no recorded review activity in RevisOp" with "not studying" — the app only observes what happens inside it, never offline study — and should be worded/thresholded accordingly from the start.
- **Status: ✅ SHIPPED — SQL deployed & verified live (15/09/2026).** `docs/database/sprint8.0/01_SCHEMA` + `02_FUNCTIONS` + `04_SCHEMA` deployed; `03_TEST` — **20/20 PASS**, `05_TEST` (trigger retirement) — pending operator run, including every `[CRITICAL]` check (no profile-field writes on join, idempotent re-request, approve/reject atomicity + admin-only guards, explicit-selection add with no guessing, bogus-group-id rejected, unapproved member blocked from `get_group_detail`, course change no longer removes existing batch membership). Frontend (`GroupJoin.jsx`, `AdminDashboard.jsx`) written against the deployed signatures.

**D-16: Batch group Active/Archived lifecycle — admin-only archive/restore, frozen report snapshot (Phase 8, Sprint 8.1, decided 15/09/2026)**
- **What:** Batch groups gain a second lifecycle dimension orthogonal to membership: `study_groups.archived_at timestamptz` (NULL = active). Admin-only `archive_batch_group(p_group_id)`/`restore_batch_group(p_group_id)`, both `SECURITY DEFINER`, both lock the batch row (`FOR UPDATE`) before acting so a concurrent enrollment action and an archive either complete first (and are honored) or see the committed archived state and are refused — same lock-first convention on both sides. Archiving, in one transaction: captures a jsonb snapshot (group metadata + `get_batch_group_member_stats`'s own rows, reusing that RPC rather than reimplementing it) into a new `batch_group_archives` table, marks `archived_at`, and closes any outstanding `'requested'`/`'invited'` membership rows to a new `'closed'` status (with `closed_at`/`closed_reason` — not deleted, not silently left as-is). Restoring clears `archived_at` and leaves approved (`'active'`) memberships and `'closed'` rows exactly as they were — a closed request is never auto-revived, only reactivated by an explicit new request through `join_group_by_token`, which now upgrades a `'closed'` row back to `'requested'` (preserving the old `closed_at`/`closed_reason` as history) instead of erroring on the unique constraint. Every enrollment-adjacent path — `join_group_by_token`, `enroll_user_in_batch_group` (direct-add), `approve_batch_join_request`, `reject_batch_join_request` — now locks the same batch row and refuses once `archived_at` is set. `get_batch_group_archive(p_group_id)` reads the snapshot matching the batch's *current* `archived_at` (so a restore-then-re-archive cycle always resolves the latest snapshot, never a stale one), gated identically to `get_batch_group_member_stats` (professor/admin/super_admin only — no broadened access). `leave_group` no longer cascade-deletes a batch group when its last active member leaves (previously true for any group) — a batch group's lifecycle is now exclusively archive/restore. `get_admin_batch_groups` gained `archived_at` (AdminDashboard splits Active/Archived client-side, cheap at 3 live batch groups); `get_my_batch_groups` (the professor/admin monitoring list on MyGroups.jsx) now excludes archived batches server-side.
- **Why:** an institution's batch eventually ends (course/cohort completes), but its historical report and its members' existing content/account access must survive that — deleting the group was never viable (kills approved memberships and shared-content access), and doing nothing left ended batches cluttering active monitoring and the invite link silently still open for new joins. A frozen snapshot (not a live re-query with a date filter) is required because the underlying report (`get_batch_group_member_stats`) computes weekly figures relative to `CURRENT_DATE` at call time — there was no existing point-in-time mechanism to reuse, so "archived" must freeze data, not just filter it.
- **Verified before building (pre-flight):** `is_batch_group` (not `group_type`) is the flag every existing admin/report/guard path already trusts — confirmed live 1:1-consistent with `group_type='batch'` on all 3 real batch groups, so D-16 uses `is_batch_group` exclusively and does not touch the separate, pre-existing `group_type` plumbing. Pre-flight also surfaced three RLS gaps with no relation to D-15/D-16's own design, closed as part of this migration since they became directly relevant to "prevent bypasses": `sg_delete_creator` and `sg_update_creator` (both on `study_groups`) had no `is_batch_group` clause at all — a batch group's creating admin could delete it, or write `archived_at` directly, entirely bypassing the snapshot transaction — narrowed to `is_batch_group = false`, since batch groups have never had a direct-edit UI to lose. `sgm_insert_admin` (INSERT on `study_group_members`) had no archived check, so a client holding local group-admin rights could insert a member into an archived batch straight past `enroll_user_in_batch_group`'s guard — closed with an `archived_at IS NOT NULL` exclusion in the policy itself.
- **Impact:** `study_group_members.status` CHECK gains `'closed'` (alongside `'invited' | 'active' | 'requested'`), plus new `closed_at`/`closed_reason` columns. New table `batch_group_archives` (RLS enabled, zero client-facing policies — reads only through `get_batch_group_archive`). Three RLS policies rewritten (see Verified-before-building). `leave_group`, `join_group_by_token`, `get_group_preview`, `enroll_user_in_batch_group`, `approve_batch_join_request`, `reject_batch_join_request`, `get_admin_batch_groups`, `get_my_batch_groups`, `get_group_detail` all `CREATE OR REPLACE`d — reproduced from their exact live bodies (`pg_get_functiondef`, confirmed during pre-flight since several had already drifted from any committed migration file) with additive, minimal diffs only. No changes to shared-content access, deletion (still fully blocked for batch groups, now via RLS + the `leave_group` fix rather than left open), or any Suspended/Retired state (explicitly out of scope this sprint).
- **Status: ✅ SHIPPED — SQL deployed & verified live (15/09/2026).** `docs/database/sprint8.1/01_SCHEMA` + `02_FUNCTIONS` + `03_FUNCTIONS` deployed by the operator; `04_TEST` — **33/33 PASS**, including every `[CRITICAL]` check (admin-only archive/restore, non-batch/missing-group rejection, snapshot capture matching timestamp, active memberships untouched, outstanding requests closed with reason recorded, idempotent repeat archive/restore, all four enrollment paths refused once archived, both narrowed RLS policies genuinely block a direct client write — confirmed via a dedicated `SET ROLE authenticated` diagnostic after the first pass gave a false FAIL from checking row-existence under a role that couldn't see the row at all, batch group survives its last member leaving, closed request not auto-revived by restore, re-request after restore reactivates the closed row, re-archive produces a new snapshot with the earlier one retained, `get_batch_group_archive` gated correctly, listing surfaces filter correctly). One post-deploy fix needed: `archive_batch_group` used `NOW()` (constant for the whole transaction) instead of `clock_timestamp()`, which collided on a restore-then-re-archive done inside one transaction — `06_FIX_archive_timestamp_precision.sql`, redeployed. Full live click-through verification also done as an authenticated admin in production (Active/Archived filter, Archive, Restore, the frozen-snapshot GroupDetail view, and the public "ended" join page), clean consoles throughout. **Quality Auditor follow-up (same day):** asked whether three specific scenarios were verified — `08_TEST_auditor_followup.sql`, **7/7 PASS**: (1) snapshot-capture failure — a transaction-scoped fault-injection trigger forces the snapshot INSERT to fail, confirms the batch stays active, the membership is untouched, and no orphaned snapshot row is created (no partial state); (2) frozen report vs. real later activity — a genuine `reviews` row inserted for a real student before archiving is captured in the snapshot, a second real review inserted *after* archiving does not change the already-saved snapshot, while the live (non-archived) reporting path correctly reflects both — proving the freeze is real, not a trivial always-empty case. **`05_TEST_concurrency_manual` (two-tab live-session lock-contention proof) remains not completed** — the manual two-tab choreography proved too fragile to coordinate reliably through this workflow (every failure was stale/misordered fixture state correctly triggering the guard, never an actual bug); an automated single-script alternative via `dblink` was considered and rejected since it would require embedding a database password. Residual, disclosed as such: the `FOR UPDATE` lock-first pattern used throughout is a standard, well-understood Postgres idiom, correct by construction, but has not been demonstrated against a genuine concurrent race. Frontend (`AdminDashboard.jsx` Active/Archived filter + Archive/Restore actions, `GroupDetail.jsx` archived-snapshot display, `GroupJoin.jsx` "This batch has ended" state) written against the deployed signatures, committed and pushed (`6bc1796`).

**Sprint 8.2 — Help documentation corrected to match D-15/D-16, plus question-type guidance (Phase 8, Group A, content-only, 16/09/2026)**
- **What:** `src/data/helpContent.js`'s seven sections still described the pre-D-15 world — automatic course+institution batch matching, "Grant Access" auto-enrolling into a batch, no mention of archive/restore at all. Rewrote `prof-welcome`, `prof-profile-setup`, `prof-batch-groups`, `prof-batch-performance`, `admin-dashboard-overview`, `admin-access-requests`, `admin-batch-groups` to describe the real Sprint 8.0/8.1 flow: an admin creates an empty batch, shares its invite link, a student requests to join, an admin approves/rejects or direct-adds one named student to one named batch (that pick is the approval), and archiving freezes the report while restore resumes live reporting without reopening closed requests. `guideContent.js` was checked and needed no change — it never made the specific false claims (confirmed by a dedicated research pass).
- **Also added, same sprint (Section B/C of the sprint brief):** a role-visible `question-types-guide` section (purpose/example/creator/dos-don'ts/how-to-answer for all 8 question types, visible to every role since students study types they can't author), a professor/admin-only `prof-question-type-authoring` section (manual-authoring mechanics for the 5 D-10-gated types), and a role-split CSV pair — `bulk-csv-basics` (all roles, since Bulk Upload itself and its flashcard/theory rows are open to students too) and a rewritten `prof-bulk-csv` (professor/admin only, the 4 gated types' CSV columns, with real example rows copied verbatim from the app's own downloadable template). Replaced the old `flashcard-creation` section's stale "front,back only, CSV is professors-only" framing.
- **Real product gotcha documented, not fixed (out of scope — content-only sprint):** `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx`'s CSV parser only recognizes `question_type` values `mcq`/`correct_incorrect`/`theory`/`case_study_mcq`/`fitb` (`RECOGNIZED_QUESTION_TYPES`) — a row with `match_the_following` or `concept_card` in that column is not rejected, it silently becomes a plain `flashcard`. Called out explicitly in the new `bulk-csv-basics` help section and logged in `docs/tracking/bugs.md`.
- **Spot-check of the three super-admin-only sections (pre-flight step, not full rewrite) found real factual drift, corrected:** `superadmin-user-roles` described a non-existent "role change dialog" and a free role picker including `super_admin` (the real UI is per-current-role "Prof"/"Admin"/"Student" buttons with no path to `super_admin` and a plain browser `prompt()` for the optional reason); `superadmin-hard-delete` named a "Delete User" button and an email-confirmation step that don't exist (the real control is an unlabeled trash icon and a "type DELETE in capital letters" `prompt()`), listed "friend connections" among deleted data (not in the real `admin_delete_user_data` scope), and omitted that the Supabase auth record survives and needs a manual follow-up delete; `superadmin-sa-analytics` had the wrong header stats, the wrong cohort-table columns, and two bullets (Retention Cards, Audit Log) that actually belong to the separate Super Admin Dashboard page, not SA Analytics.
- **Also removed:** a fabricated "Maximum 200 cards per upload" claim in the old `prof-bulk-csv` tip — confirmed via code search that no such cap exists anywhere in the upload path (client-side or RPC).
- **Verification:** `npm run build` and `npx eslint src/data/helpContent.js` both clean. Every button label, tab name, and RPC-driven behavior quoted in the new/corrected sections was confirmed against the live component source (`AdminDashboard.jsx`, `GroupJoin.jsx`, `FlashcardCreate.jsx`, `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx`, `StudyMode.jsx`, `SuperAdminDashboard.jsx`, `SuperAdminAnalytics.jsx`), not assumed from the old help text or from this decision log alone. **Not done:** an in-browser, per-role click-through of the Help page itself (student/professor/admin/super_admin, desktop + mobile) — this session had no test-account credentials for those roles; content accuracy was verified at the source-code level instead. Flagged for the operator to spot-check live.
- **Status:** ✅ Content shipped, no SQL, no schema, no new Decision Log entry (no architectural decision made — corrections only).

**Sprint 8.3 — Bug fixes + Help renderer: hyperlinks & screenshots (Phase 8, Group A, 16/09/2026)**
- **What (Part A, bug fixes):** **A1** — `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx`'s CSV parser (the gap Sprint 8.2 documented but didn't fix, `docs/tracking/bugs.md`) now rejects any non-blank, unrecognized `question_type` with a specific row error instead of silently downgrading it to a plain flashcard; a genuinely blank cell still silently defaults to `flashcard`, unchanged. **A2** — `downloadTemplate()`'s own example rows carried the same unqualified, budget-year-dependent tax figures (`₹2.5 lakhs` exemption limit, `Rs 10000` u/s 80TTA) that Sprint 8.2's auditor pass had already fixed in `helpContent.js`'s prose — replaced with timeless structural facts (direct-vs-indirect tax distinction, the 80TTA/80TTB *rule* without a figure, the 80C section number instead of an amount). `helpContent.js`'s own verbatim-copied CSV examples were deliberately left untouched (out of scope per the sprint brief), so the two files' example rows are no longer copies of each other — a cosmetic drift, not a defect.
- **What (Part B, Help renderer):** `Help.jsx`'s `ContentBlock` gained two capabilities that didn't exist before this sprint (Sprint 8.2 explicitly deferred both): **B1** — an inline `[label](/route)` link syntax, parsed at render time into a real react-router `<Link>` for any `/`-prefixed target (internal navigation only, no external-link handling); a malformed or non-`/`-prefixed match simply never matches the regex, so it degrades to plain text rather than throwing. Applied first to the Sprint 8.2 batch-lifecycle sections' "Go to Admin Dashboard"/"Go to Study Groups" plain-text mentions (`admin-dashboard-overview`, `admin-access-requests`, `admin-batch-groups`, `prof-batch-performance`). **B2** — a new `case 'image'` content-block type (`{ src, alt, caption?, annotations?: [{x, y, label}] }`), with numbered CSS-positioned circular markers over the image (percentage-based `x`/`y`, so a callout can be repositioned without re-editing the screenshot) and a corresponding numbered caption list. First use: `public/help-screenshots/` (new folder — the first static content image this codebase has ever shipped; root-relative path, Vite's own convention, confirmed against the sprint's own pre-flight check that no such pattern existed yet).
- **Screenshots actually captured (3 of the original 5-flow plan — see below for why 2 were dropped):** `admin-batch-create-invite-link.png`, `admin-batch-pending-requests.png`, `admin-batch-archive-filter.png`, all wired into `admin-batch-groups`. All three use a disposable, obviously-labeled test batch group ("TEST 2 — Demo Batch (Screenshot Sandbox, delete after)") and a dummy student test account ("TestOutlook") — no real student/professor name, email, or batch data is visible in any of the three. Captured via the operator's own OS screenshot tool (Win+Shift+S) rather than the Browser pane's screenshot action, since that tool has no way to export a screenshot as a file the session can commit — each was then resized/recompressed with `sharp` (already a project dependency) to 10-20KB apiece before being placed in `public/help-screenshots/`.
- **Deferred, not waived — 2 of the original 5-flow screenshot plan:** the "role-change confirmation" and "Hard Delete confirmation" flows (`SuperAdminDashboard.jsx`) both turned out to be plain native browser `confirm()`/`prompt()` dialogs, not custom React modals — not something the Browser pane's automation can click through (confirmed: the click silently no-ops since the dialog isn't part of the page's DOM/CDP event stream), and not something worth checking into product documentation even if captured by hand — generic, unstyled OS/browser chrome, not RevisOp UI, rendering differently per browser/OS. Per the Quality Auditor's review of this sprint's completion report: this is **not** treated as satisfying the original 5-screenshot requirement — the right fix is to replace both native dialogs with real, accessible, in-app confirmation dialogs (an independent, legitimate UX improvement for two high-consequence/destructive actions, not manufactured just to get a screenshot), then screenshot *those*. Tracked as a real backlog item in §3.2, not closed here.
- **Real bug found and fixed along the way (not originally in scope) — independently documented per the sprint's own audit:** setting up a disposable test batch to capture these screenshots surfaced that `create_batch_group` never wrote `group_type='batch'` on the rows it creates — silently disabling D-15's student-approval gate for every batch group created through the live function (a dummy student joined instantly active, no pending step, no approval). Confirmed via 2 diagnostic SQL scripts, run by the operator, that all 3 real production batches were unaffected (already `group_type='batch'`, predating whatever change introduced this). Fixed via a separate, dedicated SQL file (not folded into the frontend commit's prose) and independently verified live afterward. Full code/SQL diff, diagnostic queries, and verification steps: `docs/database/sprint8.3/00-03_*.sql` + its own `docs/tracking/bugs.md` entry — kept separable from this sprint's approved frontend scope, since a useful discovery found mid-task should stay auditable on its own, not get absorbed into "things this sprint shipped."
- **Verification:** `npm run build` and `npx eslint` clean on all four touched files. A1's gate logic verified via a standalone extraction run against 8 cases (all passed); A2's corrected template rows verified to still parse to the correct column count via the app's own `parseCSVLine`, copied verbatim into the same standalone test. B1 live-verified in the Browser pane (happy-path navigation) **and**, per the Quality Auditor's follow-up request, its malformed-input handling was verified separately against 17 cases (unclosed brackets, external URLs, reversed brackets, nested brackets, empty label/target, non-string inputs, mixed valid+broken in one string) — all 17 degraded to plain text or partial-link output with zero crashes. Desktop + mobile (375px) layouts checked, no overflow, no console errors. The `group_type` fix was live-verified end-to-end: after the fix, a fresh disposable test batch + a dummy student account correctly produced a "Pending Batch Requests" row instead of instant membership.
- **Not done, disclosed as a real test limitation (Quality Auditor review), not "fully verified":** A1/A2's specified end-to-end CSV upload test was replaced by extracted-logic testing — the Browser pane tool available this session has no file-upload capability, so the actual `<input type="file">` → `parseCSV` → Supabase insert path was never exercised live, only the pure parsing/validation logic in isolation. This is a real gap in the original test plan, not equivalent to a live upload, and should be closed with an actual file-upload click-through the next time this code is touched. PII check on all 3 screenshots was a manual visual read by both the session and the operator before committing, per the sprint's explicit requirement.
- **Status:** Implementation complete, **audit close pending 2 housekeeping items** per the Quality Auditor's disposition (16/09/2026): (1) delete/archive the 2 disposable test batch groups still sitting in production — tracked below; (2) the deferred role-change/Hard Delete screenshots recorded as a real backlog item (§3.2), not silently dropped. No new architectural Decision Log entry (bug fixes + a renderer feature, not a design decision).

**Quality Auditor review of Sprint 8.3's completion report (16/09/2026)** — disposition: A1 accepted with the test-limitation caveat above recorded honestly rather than claimed as full verification; A2 accepted as-is; B1 accepted once malformed-syntax coverage was actually demonstrated (done, see Verification above — the original report only showed a happy-path click); B2's renderer architecture (root-relative `public/help-screenshots/`, responsive images, semantic `alt`, percentage-based CSS-positioned annotations) accepted as a scalable first pattern; screenshots 1-3 accepted including the PII check; screenshots 4-5 explicitly deferred, not waived, pending a real accessible-dialog replacement for the two native `confirm()`/`prompt()` flows; the `group_type` bug fix accepted as a good catch, on the condition that it stays independently documented rather than folded into the approved scope's own count (done, see above); the 2 test batches required to be cleaned up before the sprint is considered operationally closed (see below).

**Pre-flight Step 0 for Sprint 8.4 — `group_type`/`is_batch_group` consistency audit (carried in from Sprint 8.3's finding, 16/09/2026):** every read/write of both columns on `study_groups`, frontend and backend, was grepped. No second instance of the "set one column, not the other" bug shape (the one already fixed in `create_batch_group`) was found in any live write path. `is_batch_group` is the de facto canonical flag — read by nearly everything (RLS-adjacent guards, archiving, admin/report RPCs, cascade-delete exemption, most UI branching); `group_type`'s 3-way enum is read in exactly two places (`join_group_by_token`'s approval-gate branch, `GroupJoin.jsx`'s copy/labels), both needing the 3rd `system_course`/`custom` state a boolean can't carry. **Collapsing the two fields was not attempted this sprint** — the audit found two live RPCs the frontend actually calls, `create_study_group` and `get_user_groups`, with no matching definition anywhere in the repo's SQL docs (`CreateGroup.jsx`/`MyGroups.jsx` call parameters/columns absent from every filed version, meaning newer, unfiled versions are live in Supabase). A schema change without visibility into those two bodies would be exactly the blind change the sprint's own capped-scope instruction says to stop and report on. If pursued later: pull both functions' live definitions via `pg_get_functiondef` first.

**D-17: Exam date capture via first-login popup, not signup — permanent dismiss (Phase 8, Sprint 8.4, decided/deployed 16/09/2026)**
- **What:** Two nullable columns on `profiles` — `exam_date` (exact date, once known) and `exam_month` (1st-of-month placeholder, used only before an exact date is announced) — plus `has_dismissed_exam_prompt` (boolean, default false). Display precedence: `exam_date` set → exact days-remaining countdown; only `exam_month` set → text only ("Exam: November 2026"), never a fabricated days-count; neither set → CTA. Single value per student (Phase B multi-course remains deferred, unaffected). Editable indefinitely from Profile Settings, including month→exact upgrade. **No backfill** — every pre-existing student sees the CTA/popup exactly like a new student who skipped it, matching the project's own D-15 "never guess what should be an explicit action" principle.
- **Why (scope changed twice from the original sprint brief, both by explicit operator decision during pre-flight, not by drift):** (1) `profiles` row creation on signup goes through `handle_new_user()`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` trigger reading `raw_user_meta_data` — because email-confirmation signup returns no client session, `auth.uid()` is null at that moment, so any client-side write (including a follow-up `UPDATE` right after `signUp()`) would silently fail RLS. Capturing `exam_month` in `Signup.jsx` as originally scoped would have required extending that trigger too. The operator chose instead to drop the `Signup.jsx` picker entirely and capture exam info via a dismissible popup shown on first login (any student, new or pre-existing) — by then a real session exists, so an ordinary `profiles` update works, no trigger change needed. (2) Popup dismissal is permanent (`has_dismissed_exam_prompt`), matching the existing `has_dismissed_goal_prompt` pattern, rather than re-appearing every login — the nav chip and Dashboard CTA remain as the passive ongoing reminder after a skip.
- **Verified before building:** `profiles` had no exam-date column, confirmed live via `information_schema.columns` (not docs alone — which were also found to be missing the pre-existing, unrelated `access_request_ref`/`updated_at` columns, now added to `DATABASE_SCHEMA.md`). Students remain single-course (`now.md`'s "Phase B: Student Multi-Course" still deferred). `users_update_own_profile` RLS (`id = auth.uid()`) already covers the new columns — no RLS change needed.
- **Impact:** New `ExamDateContext.jsx` (fetch-once + `saveExamDate`/`dismissPrompt` mutators, same shape as `CourseContext.jsx`), consumed by a new dismissible `ExamDatePromptModal.jsx` (shown from `Dashboard.jsx` only after the onboarding/profile-completion modals have both cleared, so dialogs never stack), a new `ExamDateChip.jsx` in the nav (student-only, joins `StudyTimerChip` in both `NavDesktop.jsx`/`NavMobile.jsx`), a self-gating "Days Until Your Exam" Dashboard card (renders only when an exact date exists), and a new Exam Date section in `ProfileSettings.jsx`. Shared date-math lives in `src/lib/examDate.js`, parsing `YYYY-MM-DD` values directly and doing UTC-anchored day-count arithmetic — deliberately never `toISOString()` or a timezone-local `Date` read, the exact footgun this project's own DB rules warn against elsewhere (`reviews.next_review_date`).
- **Status: ✅ SHIPPED — SQL deployed & verified live (16/09/2026).** `docs/database/sprint8.4/00_DIAGNOSTIC` + `01_SCHEMA` deployed by the operator. `npm run build` clean across all new/changed files including lazy-loaded chunks. Live click-through in the Browser pane as a real pre-existing student (`TestOutlook`, no exam info set — confirms the no-backfill CTA behavior genuinely, not just in theory): popup on first login; month-only save (November 2026) → chip shows month text, no dashboard card; upgrade to an exact date (15/11/2026) via Profile Settings → chip updates to "60d left" **immediately, no reload**, dashboard card appears with matching count/date; changed the date again (20/12/2026) → clean update to "95d left" everywhere, no stale state; mobile nav (375px) checked, no overflow. Day-count math verified correct against the calendar. Unrelated fix made while verifying: `.claude/launch.json`'s dev-server port mismatch (`vite` ignored the harness's assigned proxy port whenever the default port was taken) — pinned to an explicit `--port 5183 --strictPort`.
- **Follow-up addition, iteration 1 (operator request, same day):** a month-only student (exam_month set, no exam_date) gets a slim text nudge on the Dashboard — "Exam expected [Month Year] — date announced? Update it" — linking to Profile Settings, distinct from the countdown card above (which stays exact-date-only). An automated announcement-detection notification was evaluated and declined in the same pass — the app has no way to know when ICAI/the board announces a date, and it's explicitly out of scope per the sprint brief.
- **Follow-up addition, iteration 2 (operator request, same day) — D-17 precision rule amended, not overridden:** the operator asked to add a days-count for month-only students after all, explicitly framed as "tentative" for early urgency — a materially different ask than the flat countdown declined in iteration 1, since the original objection was to an *unlabeled* number that reads as certain, not to showing an estimate as an estimate. Approved with one condition: the "tentative" qualifier must sit directly beside the number, not in a caption underneath, since a caveat below a bold number gets skimmed past while the number itself doesn't. Implemented as an upgrade to the same nudge line (not the countdown card, and not the nav chip — both stay as iteration 1 left them, decided explicitly to avoid the chip's limited space dropping the qualifier exactly where it matters): `~{days} days` computed from `exam_month` (already stored as the 1st of the month, so the existing `daysUntilExamDate` needed no change) immediately followed by `(tentative — assumes the 1st of {Month Year})` in the same line, then the "Update once announced" link. Live-verified: `~227 days (tentative — assumes the 1st of May 2027)` rendered correctly for a real month-only profile, math confirmed correct against the calendar (16/09/2026 → 01/05/2027); nav chip unaffected (still month-text only); `npm run build` clean.
- **Follow-up addition, iteration 3 (audit reframe, same day) — supersedes iteration 2's "tentative" hedge with a genuinely truthful count instead of a hedged estimate:** review found iteration 2's number, hedge and all, was still implicitly framed as an estimate of *days until the exam* — and a caveat doesn't change what a number is measuring, only how apologetically it's presented. The stronger fix: change the referent entirely, to something that needs no hedge because it isn't an estimate — **days until the stated calendar month begins**, which is exact arithmetic on a known fact, not a guess about an unannounced exam date. Restructured the same nudge block (still un-carded, per the earlier "lower visual weight matches lower certainty" decision) into a labeled 3-line form: `EXAM RUNWAY · {Month Year}` / `{days} days until {Month} begins` / `Exact exam date not yet set · Update once announced` — no "~", no "tentative", because there's nothing being estimated. Same underlying `daysUntilExamDate(examMonth)` call as iteration 2; only the label and framing changed. A revision-phase indicator (e.g. "Phase: Build & Strengthen" → "Intensive Revision" → "Final Consolidation" as the runway shortens) was proposed as a natural extension but explicitly deferred — it needs real pedagogical judgment on phase boundaries and per-phase guidance, not an inline implementation decision; logged in §3.2. Live-verified: `EXAM RUNWAY · MAY 2027` / `227 days until May begins` / `Exact exam date not yet set · Update once announced` rendered correctly; `npm run build` clean.
- **Approved extension to D-17 (audit review, 16/09/2026):** `has_dismissed_exam_prompt` and `ExamDatePromptModal` were flagged by review as a genuine, database-persisted product/schema decision beyond the sprint brief's original two-nullable-column contract ("student sees the CTA when no date exists" → "student can permanently suppress the popup"), not incidental UI polish, even though the popup itself was already an operator-approved pivot from the brief's original `Signup.jsx`-picker plan (made mid-sprint for the `handle_new_user()` trigger reason above). Formally recorded here as an approved extension rather than left implicit in the scope-pivot narrative, on the condition its lifecycle be explicitly verified before commit — not assumed correct from the implementation alone. **Verified live (16/09/2026)**, reusing the existing `TestOutlook` test profile reset to a pristine state via `docs/database/sprint8.4/02_FIX_reset_exam_prompt_dismissal_test_account.sql` (scoped by email, safe to rerun, no second account needed): (1) `has_dismissed_exam_prompt=false`/`exam_date`/`exam_month` both `NULL` → popup correctly reappeared on login; (2) "Skip for now" → modal closed, no error; (3) reload → popup did **not** reappear, confirming the dismiss is permanent, not per-session; (4) nav chip showed "Set exam date" CTA both before and after the dismiss — confirms the flag gates only the modal, never the chip; (5) Profile Settings' Exam Date section remained fully reachable and functional throughout, unaffected by the dismissed flag; (6) set an exact date (05/05/2027) afterward → clean update, chip → "231d left", toast "Exam date updated", no stale/duplicate state, no interference from the prior dismissal. All 6 checks passed with zero code changes required.

**D-18: Offline study-log category is chosen at stop/log time, required, five fixed values, nullable/no-backfill column (Phase 8, Sprint 8.5, decided 16/09/2026 — SQL not yet deployed)**
- **What:** Nullable `category text` on `study_sessions`, `CHECK`-constrained to exactly five values — `reading`, `writing_practice`, `lecture_viewing`, `paper_solving`, `mock_test` — applies only to manually-logged offline sessions (`source = 'manual'`). No default, no backfill: every pre-existing row simply has no category, same D-15 "never guess at historical data" principle Sprint 8.4 followed for `exam_date`/`exam_month`. Category is chosen when a session is stopped/logged, not when it's started — a single frictionless Start click matters more for habit formation than upfront categorization, and "what did I just do" is a more honest question than "what am I about to do" for a session that runs long or turns into something else. Required to complete logging — a session cannot save with no category.
- **Why exactly five, not six:** the brief's original sixth option, "Revision/Recap," was dropped before building — it mixed a purpose/timing axis (first pass vs. revisiting) into an activity-type axis (what you did), creating real overlap with every other category rather than a clean sixth bucket. A first-pass-vs-revision signal, if wanted later, belongs as an orthogonal flag on any category, not a category of its own — logged in §3.2, not built.
- **Gap the brief didn't cover, resolved by asking rather than assuming:** the brief scoped the picker in detail for `StudyTimerWidget.jsx`'s card but never addressed the nav-bar `StudyTimerChip.jsx`, which previously stopped, logged, and toasted a confirmation in a single tap with no card to host a picker in. Making category required meant that instant-log path could no longer complete instantly. Asked the operator directly rather than picking silently: chosen fix is that the chip's tap now navigates to `/dashboard/study-time`, reusing the exact pattern the app already uses for the 4-16h recovery case, rather than building a new chip-anchored popover.
- **Impact:** `StudyTimerContext.jsx`'s `stopAndLog()` no longer writes to the DB — it finalizes the duration into a new `pendingLog` state, persisted to its own `revisop_manual_timer_pending_log` localStorage key (mirroring the existing running-timer/recovery persistence) so a reload mid-picker re-shows the picker instead of silently losing an already-stopped session. A new `confirmCategory(category)` performs the actual insert once chosen. `StudyTimerWidget.jsx` gained a 5-option picker (Save disabled until a selection) shown identically whether reached via the direct stop path or a resolved recovery choice (full-time or custom-hours). `StudyTimerChip.jsx` navigates on the new `needs_category` outcome. `get_study_time_stats` needed zero changes — confirmed via `pg_get_functiondef` that its aggregation is purely `SUM(duration_seconds)`, no category reference.
- **Status: ✅ SHIPPED — SQL deployed & verified live (16/09/2026).** `00_DIAGNOSTIC` confirmed the live `study_sessions` shape matched docs exactly (8 columns, no `category`) and that `get_study_time_stats` is purely `SUM(duration_seconds)` with zero category references, before `01_SCHEMA` added the column. `02_TEST_verify_category_column.sql` run by the operator in the Supabase SQL Editor, in two parts (the editor stops at the first error, and section 4 is designed to error): sections 1-3 confirmed the column exists as `text`/nullable, zero pre-existing rows carry a category, and a valid insert (`category = 'reading'`) succeeds — all rolled back, nothing persisted; section 4 confirmed an invalid value (`'not_a_real_category'`) is rejected with `23514 check_violation` on `study_sessions_category_check`. One correction made mid-verification: the test file originally used `auth.uid()` for the write checks, which is always `NULL` in the SQL Editor's no-session context and caused a `user_id NOT NULL` violation on the first attempt (caught safely by the same `BEGIN`/`ROLLBACK` wrapper, nothing left behind) — rewritten to look up the project's existing disposable test account (`anandmore@outlook.com` / TestOutlook, the same account Sprint 8.4's `02_FIX_reset_exam_prompt_dismissal_test_account.sql` scopes by email) via `profiles.email` instead. Frontend was already live-verified in the Browser pane before deployment (picker renders/validates correctly, `pendingLog` survives a full page reload, the pre-existing <10s no-op path unchanged, and a pre-deployment Save attempt correctly surfaced `PGRST204 "category column not found"` — proving the SQL-first gate worked as intended). No frontend changes needed post-deployment.
- **Addendum — DB-level enforcement added before commit, quality-auditor finding (16/09/2026):** the frontend requires a category, but nothing in the database did — `category` is nullable and its own `CHECK` only validates a value when one is present, so a future write path outside `confirmCategory()` (a bulk import, a different form, a regression) could still insert `source='manual', category=NULL` and silently reopen the exact gap this sprint closes. Added a second, separate constraint: `ALTER TABLE study_sessions ADD CONSTRAINT study_sessions_manual_requires_category CHECK (source <> 'manual' OR category IS NOT NULL) NOT VALID` (`docs/database/sprint8.5/04_SCHEMA_manual_requires_category.sql`) — chosen as a `NOT VALID` CHECK over a trigger because every other rule on this table (`source`'s allowed values, `duration_seconds > 0`, `category`'s own value set) is already a plain `CHECK`, and triggers elsewhere in this app are reserved for cross-table side effects (counters, badges), not single-row validation. `NOT VALID` enforces on every future INSERT/UPDATE without scanning or rewriting rows that already existed — no backfill, no global `NOT NULL`. Before relying on "old rows are simply never touched again" as the reason this can never break a historical row, immutability was verified live rather than trusted from the doc alone (`03_DIAGNOSTIC_confirm_study_sessions_immutable.sql`, 16/09/2026): the only RLS policies on `study_sessions` are `INSERT`/`SELECT` for `authenticated` (no `UPDATE`), no function in the `public` schema references this table alongside an `UPDATE` statement, and no trigger is attached to the table itself — rows are genuinely write-once, so "also enforced on future UPDATEs" is a non-issue here. `05_TEST_verify_manual_requires_category.sql` proved all three required cases live: a `NULL`-category manual insert is rejected (`23514` on `study_sessions_manual_requires_category`), a valid categorized manual insert still succeeds, and 3 real pre-existing March 2026 manual rows with `category IS NULL` read back untouched. The one non-`ROLLBACK`ed test row (needed to see its own `RETURNING` result cleanly) was removed via a tightly-scoped preview-then-delete (`06_CLEANUP_remove_test_row.sql`), confirmed to match exactly 1 row before deletion. No frontend changes required — `confirmCategory()` already only ever sends one of the 5 valid values.

**D-19: Offline study-log minimum raised from 10 seconds to 10 minutes, enforced server-side (Phase 8, Sprint 8.6a, decided 17/09/2026 — SQL not yet deployed)**
- **What:** `StudyTimerContext.jsx`'s `stopAndLog()` floor changed from `durationSeconds < 10` to `durationSeconds < 600` (10 minutes) via a new `MIN_LOGGABLE_SECONDS` constant. Below the floor, `stopAndLog()` now returns a distinct `'too_short'` outcome instead of the previous `{ outcome: 'logged', durationSeconds: 0 }` — the old behavior would render "Session logged: 0s" for what might be a genuine short attempt, which reads as a bug even though it was deliberate no-op behavior. Mirrored server-side by a new `study_sessions_duration_floor` `CHECK (duration_seconds >= 600) NOT VALID` constraint, same rationale and same `NOT VALID`/prospective-only pattern as D-18's `study_sessions_manual_requires_category` — the frontend already stops a sub-threshold session from ever reaching the insert, but nothing in the database backstopped that on its own.
- **Why 10 minutes, framed as scope not judgment:** user-facing copy for the new `'too_short'` outcome reads "RevisOp records offline study sessions of 10 minutes or more — shorter moments aren't included in manual study-time tracking," deliberately never "not serious enough" — the floor exists because sub-10-minute manual entries are too noisy/unreliable to be meaningful study-time signal, not a judgment on whether a short session counted as real studying.
- **No backfill:** existing sub-threshold historical rows (logged when the floor was 10 seconds) are untouched — same no-rewrite-of-history principle as every other `NOT VALID` constraint on this table (D-18 and its addendum).
- **Impact:** `StudyTimerContext.jsx` (floor + outcome), `StudyTimerWidget.jsx` and `StudyTimerChip.jsx` (new `'too_short'` toast branch each, since both are independent stop entry points into the same `stopAndLog()`). The 4-16h recovery-prompt path (`handleRecoveryFull`/`handleRecoveryCustom`) cannot produce `'too_short'` — its minimum possible duration is 1 hour (custom-hours input floor) — confirmed rather than assumed, not exercised by new tests. `get_study_time_stats` needed no changes — purely `SUM(duration_seconds)`, unaffected by which rows exist.
- **Status: ✅ SHIPPED — SQL deployed & verified live (17/09/2026).** `00_DIAGNOSTIC` confirmed `duration_seconds` carried only the pre-existing `study_sessions_duration_seconds_check` (`> 0`) — `NOT NULL`, no floor yet — so the new constraint's predicate needed no `IS NULL` guard. `01_SCHEMA` added `study_sessions_duration_floor`. `02_TEST` proved all three required cases live: a 599s insert rejected (`23514 check_violation` on `study_sessions_duration_floor`), a 600s insert succeeded, and 3 real pre-existing March 2026 manual rows (17s, 49s, 263s — logged when the floor was 10 seconds) read back unchanged, confirming `NOT VALID`'s prospective-only guarantee held.

**Sprint 8.6b — Grouped-Row CSV Import: match_the_following + concept_card bulk upload (Phase 8, decided/shipped 17/09/2026 — no SQL, both question_type values already live)**
- **What:** `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx` no longer defers `match_the_following` (7.8-C) or `concept_card` (7.12-C) bulk upload — both were the only two question types left without a CSV path. Neither fits the flat `option_1..4`/`correct_option` convention (match's left/right pairs and concept_card's key terms are both variable-length lists of *pairs*, and a second-level in-cell delimiter would silently mis-split real content containing its own colons/commas), so instead of forcing a flat cell, each pair gets its own CSV row: `match_group`/`match_left`/`match_right` for match_the_following, `concept_group`/`concept_term`/`concept_definition` for concept_card. The grouping *mechanic* — an uploader-chosen label column, identical across every row of one card, unique to it within the file — mirrors `case_study_mcq`'s `case_group` exactly, precisely as the pre-flight brief called for. The fan *target* is what differs: `case_group` keeps every row its own separate card, sharing only a `batch_id`; `match_group`/`concept_group` fuse every row in the group into ONE `flashcards` row before insert, since both types' DB representation genuinely is a single row (`options` = one `{left,right,correct}` object / one `{term,definition}[]` array, not N rows).
- **Grouping/validation:** `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx` accumulates rows per group during the parse pass (order-independent — grouped rows don't need to be contiguous in the file), checking `target_course`/`subject`/`topic`/`front` (and `back`, for concept_card, since unlike every other bulk-uploadable type its `back_text` is typed directly rather than derived) stay identical across every row claiming the same group id — a mismatch is rejected with a specific error naming the row and the earlier row it conflicts with, never silently merged. After the full file is read, each group is resolved through the exact same library functions manual authoring uses (`buildMatchOptions`/`deriveMatchBackText`/`validateMatchPairs` from `src/lib/matchTheFollowing.js`; `buildConceptOptions`/`validateConceptTerms` from `src/lib/conceptCard.js`), so a CSV-built card and a manually-authored card are byte-identical in the DB. match_the_following pairing is positional (row N's `match_left` matches row N's `match_right`) — the CSV format has no way to author a distractor right-item pointing at a different row's left item; that still needs manual authoring's arbitrary-remapping dropdown. 2-8 rows per match_group (`MATCH_MIN_PAIRS`/`MATCH_MAX_PAIRS`), 1-10 rows per concept_group (`CONCEPT_MIN_TERMS`/`CONCEPT_MAX_TERMS`) — same caps manual authoring enforces.
- **Gating unchanged:** match_the_following stays D-10-gated (professor/admin/super_admin only, same RESTRICTIVE RLS pair every other verdict-bearing type uses) — added to the CSV's professor-only type list and the `42501` RLS-rejection error message. concept_card stays ungated (D-06 — no authoring-judgment risk when nothing is ever graded), the only bulk-uploadable type available to every role.
- **Regression guard:** `RECOGNIZED_QUESTION_TYPES` grew from 5 to 7 values; every pre-existing flat-row type (flashcard/theory/mcq/correct_incorrect/case_study_mcq/fitb) is untouched — confirmed via a standalone Node harness (outside React/browser) exercising 5 scenarios: a valid 3-pair match card, a valid 3-term concept card, an orphan match_group (1 row, correctly rejected by the existing `validateMatchPairs` minimum), a match_group id reused across two unrelated front-texts (correctly split — the 2 genuinely matching rows still form a valid card, the 3rd conflicting row is rejected on its own, never silently absorbed), and a flat plain-flashcard row (unaffected).
- **Live verification (17/09/2026, dev server → live Supabase, real professor session, operator logged in directly — Claude never saw or entered a password):** since the Browser pane's automation has no file-picker/file-upload action to drive the CSV `<input type="file">` directly, verification instead exercised the exact insert path `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx` uses (same library calls, same row shape) via the authenticated page's own module scope — a 3-pair match_the_following card and a 3-term concept_card both inserted successfully (professor RLS gate confirmed permissive for match_the_following), read back with byte-identical `options`/`back_text` shape to manual authoring's own insert, appeared correctly in My Study Sets, and the concept_card's "Read Concepts" viewer (`ConceptCardViewer.jsx`, unmodified by this sprint) rendered all 3 term/definition pairs correctly. Both test rows deleted after verification, reverified at 0 rows via a `front_text ILIKE` sweep.
- **Docs:** `helpContent.js` gained a new "Bulk CSV Upload — Grouped-Row Types" section (real grouped examples, header + full rows, matching the existing CSV-documentation standard) and corrected two stale claims — the "not supported, create individually" line for both types, and a tip that (incorrectly, pre-existing) said an unrecognized `question_type` silently downgrades to a plain flashcard when the code has actually rejected it outright since Sprint 8.3's A1 fix. `DATABASE_SCHEMA.md`'s match_the_following/concept_card Representation sections updated from "deferred" to describe the shipped mechanic.

**D-20: mcq_multi — multi-select MCQ, its own question_type (Phase 8, Sprint 8.6c, decided 17/09/2026 — SQL not yet deployed)**
- **What:** A new, distinct `question_type` value, `mcq_multi` — not a flag on `mcq` — because the interaction/grading flow genuinely differs: `mcq` is single-tap-immediate-verdict (`handleMcqSelect`), `mcq_multi` is build-up-then-explicit-submit (mirrors `handleMatchSubmit`, confirmed in pre-flight). Same precedent this project already follows for `case_study_mcq` getting its own type value while sharing grading machinery with `mcq`.
- **Grading:** exact-set correctness, all-or-none — no partial credit, no per-option marks, no negative marking, no configurable "minimum X selections," no weighted answers, no exam-specific scoring. Compared as parsed sets at grading time, never as raw strings.
- **Storage — canonicalize on write, verify as a set on read:** at write time (authoring `FlashcardCreate.jsx` AND CSV import `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx`), every correct-index selection is parsed, validated (unique, in-range), sorted ascending, and joined with a single semicolon and no spaces — `{4,0,2}` always serializes to exactly `"0;2;4"`. Stored in `correct_answer` (reusing the column, no migration — this schema's established idiom for every other verdict-bearing type). `src/lib/mcq.js` gained `canonicalizeMultiAnswer`/`parseMultiAnswer`/`compactMcqMultiOptions`/`validateMcqMultiOptions`/`deriveMcqMultiBackText` so both entry paths call the same functions — they cannot drift.
- **Validity rule:** hard requirement is 2-6 total options and at least 1 marked correct — NOT "at least 1 incorrect." That's a pedagogical preference, not a logical requirement, and hard-blocking it on a course-agnostic platform (see `project_identity` — RevisOp is not CA/CMA/CS-specific) bakes in an assumption real content elsewhere might not share. If every option is marked correct, `FlashcardCreate.jsx` shows a non-blocking inline notice ("All options are marked correct — confirm this is intentional") and lets the author proceed.
- **Attempt evidence:** `review_events` gained a nullable `selected_answer jsonb` column, populated only by `mcq_multi` for now (every other of the 8 existing types stays `NULL` — no backfill, no new analytics feature this sprint). `apply_review` gained exactly one new trailing parameter, `p_selected_answer jsonb DEFAULT NULL`, added via `CREATE OR REPLACE` against the exact live signature captured by `00_DIAGNOSTIC` (unchanged since Sprint 7.4) — confirmed post-deploy to still resolve to exactly one overload, the same overloaded-RPC/PostgREST-ambiguity risk this project was already bitten by once (§1.11, `delete_notification`/`mark_single_notification_read`).
- **Gating:** D-10-gated, professor/admin/super_admin only — added to `chk_flashcards_question_type` (9th live value) and both RESTRICTIVE policies (`flashcards_gate_verdict_types_insert`/`_update`), same `is_professor_or_admin()` check every other verdict-bearing type uses.
- **Why now, precisely:** not direct professor demand — nobody asked for multi-select MCQ by name. The actual basis: students given a blank canvas tend to become content consumers rather than content creators, so RevisOp's current rollout strategy is professor-led content creation — equipping professors with a broadly capable authoring toolkit before serious content onboarding begins, so content production doesn't later have to pause for a missing common question format. Distinct from the D-12/D-14 pattern (types *removed* for confirmed zero real usage) — this is a deliberate platform-completeness investment tied to an observed, specific behavioral pattern, not speculative build-ahead-of-any-evidence. Retroactive note: this was part of the original Phase 7 candidate scope, dropped without a recorded reason at the time.
- **StudyMode.jsx:** new branch (checkbox build-up via `AnswerOption`'s existing but previously-unused `selected` state — no new component needed, confirmed in pre-flight), rendered like `match_the_following`/`fitb` — its own branch, NOT added to `GRADED_QUESTION_TYPES` (that array is specifically the shared single-tap `AnswerOption` list). `handleRating`/`submitReview` both gained a threaded-through `selectedAnswer` parameter (default `null`) so every other question type's existing call sites are unaffected.
- **Status: ✅ SHIPPED — SQL deployed & verified live (17/09/2026).** `00_DIAGNOSTIC` confirmed the live CHECK constraint, both D-10 policy bodies, and the exact 5-param `apply_review` signature matched this sprint's assumptions exactly, before deploying. Two real bugs surfaced and fixed during deployment, not just assumed away:
  1. **`02_FUNCTIONS`'s own header claim was wrong.** It claimed appending a defaulted trailing parameter to `apply_review` via `CREATE OR REPLACE` "replaces it in place; does NOT create a second overload" — false. Running it alone left TWO live `apply_review` functions (the old 5-arg one, untouched, plus the new 6-arg one), confirmed live by `03_TEST` throwing `ERROR 42725: function public.apply_review(...) is not unique`. This is the identical overloaded-RPC/PostgREST-ambiguity class already documented at §1.11 for `get_browsable_decks` v5 — fixed the same way, with an explicit `DROP FUNCTION` of the old 5-arg signature (`docs/database/sprint8.6c/02b_HOTFIX_drop_ambiguous_apply_review_overload.sql`), run immediately after `02_FUNCTIONS`.
  2. **`03_TEST`'s own RLS-impersonation technique was incomplete.** It set `request.jwt.claims` (for `auth.uid()`) but never switched the actual Postgres session ROLE — the Supabase SQL Editor connects as a superuser/table-owner role, which bypasses Row-Level Security entirely regardless of jwt claims. This produced a false FAIL on the "student blocked from mcq_multi insert" check (the student insert silently succeeded because RLS was never actually being enforced in that test run) — the underlying gate itself was correct the whole time, confirmed by directly reading the live policy body mid-diagnosis. Fixed by adding `SET LOCAL ROLE authenticated;` around every RLS-relevant statement, the same idiom `sprint7.5/02_TEST_verify_d10_role_gate.sql` already established for this exact reason — should have been followed from the start instead of re-deriving a weaker technique. Re-run after the fix: all 7 checks PASS, including the student-rejection check now genuinely exercising RLS.
  Final live-verified state: exactly one `apply_review` overload; professor can insert `mcq_multi`; student is genuinely RLS-rejected; `p_selected_answer` writes correctly to `review_events.selected_answer`; the pre-existing 5-arg call path (every other question type's call site) still works and leaves `selected_answer` NULL; `submit_review`'s compat wrapper still resolves and works.
- **Live browser verification (17/09/2026, dev server → live Supabase, real professor session):** manually authored two `mcq_multi` cards — one with a partial-correct set (tests the all-correct-not-required + wrong-answer paths) and one with every option marked correct (tests the non-blocking "All options are marked correct — confirm this is intentional" notice, which correctly did not block saving). `back_text` derived correctly for both (`"Life insurance premium • PPF contribution"`, every correct option joined with `•`). **Unprompted confirmation:** the professor dashboard's "Accuracy by question type" widget (`get_educator_accuracy_by_qtype`) picked up "Multi-select MCQ" automatically with zero code changes, proving the type-generalization holds the same way it did for every prior question-type sprint. Studied both cards: the partial-correct submission correctly auto-graded Hard with a single Continue button ("Marked as Hard — you'll see this again soon"); the fully-correct submission correctly revealed both options as "Correct answer" and offered the full Hard/Medium/Easy `GradeButtonRow` self-grade (graded Easy, session completed). Browse Study Sets' Question Type filter correctly listed and narrowed to "Multi-select MCQ". **CSV upload verified via a synthetic `File`/`DataTransfer` injection** (the Browser pane has no native file-picker automation, same limitation noted in Sprint 8.6b) — an invalid row (`correct_option` index 9, out of a 4-option range) was correctly rejected with `Row 3: correct_option contains an index outside the option range (1-4)` and the WHOLE file was rejected (0 rows created, confirmed via Cards Published count unchanged), then a corrected single-row CSV uploaded successfully and produced a `back_text` byte-identical to manual authoring's own output. All 4 test cards deleted post-verification (3 via SQL — the Browser pane's sandbox suppresses the native `confirm()` dialog the in-app delete button relies on, confirmed via console warning — one CSV row's invalid attempt created nothing to delete), reverified at 0 rows via a `front_text ILIKE 'SPRINT8.6C%'` sweep (`docs/database/sprint8.6c/04_CLEANUP_remove_verification_test_cards.sql`).

**D-21: Content provenance architecture (Phase 8, Sprint 8.7.1 DB foundation + Sprint 8.7.2/8.7.3 frontend restore, 18/09/2026 — SHIPPED)**
- **What:** Content provenance architecture established. Flashcard provenance is batch-level via `flashcard_batch_provenance` (one row per `batch_id`, `content_source_type` ∈ `official_body`/`original_creator` + `content_source_name`; absence of a row = legacy/unknown, read via `LEFT JOIN`, never backfilled — a row exists only when provenance exists, so its columns are `NOT NULL`, not partially-populated). Note provenance is row-level (`notes.content_source_type`/`content_source_name`, enforced only on INSERT via `fn_require_note_provenance`/`trg_require_note_provenance` — a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger, deliberately not a `CHECK`, so legacy NULL-provenance notes stay editable: a `CHECK` is re-evaluated on every `UPDATE`, so one strict enough to force provenance on insert would also block a harmless edit to a pre-8.7.1 note, and one loose enough to permit that edit would equally permit `NULL` provenance on a brand-new insert).
- **Explicitly separate from `content_creator_id`:** provenance (what source this content is based on) and `content_creator_id` (who economically owns/created it — revenue attribution) are different axes; this sprint does not touch `content_creator_id`.
- **Database enforcement foundation shipped in 8.7.1:** `flashcard_batch_provenance` (RLS enabled, zero policies for `authenticated`/`anon` — writable only through the RPC below), the extended flashcards INSERT policy (`"Users can insert their own flashcards"` now additionally requires `EXISTS(... flashcard_batch_provenance WHERE batch_id = flashcards.batch_id)`), and the atomic SECURITY DEFINER RPC `create_flashcard_batches(p_source_type, p_source_name, p_batches jsonb)` — one call can mint multiple `batch_id`s sharing one declared source (`case_study_mcq` uploads mint one `batch_id` per case group), all-or-nothing (any invalid card rolls back the whole call, including any provenance rows already inserted in that call).
- **SECURITY DEFINER hardening — RLS does not see this function at all:** the function owner is `postgres`, which owns `flashcards`/`notes`/`profiles`, and Step 0's diagnostic confirmed `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` is not set on either table — so as a `SECURITY DEFINER` function running as table owner, `create_flashcard_batches` is completely invisible to every RLS policy, including the D-10 verdict-type gate and the ownership check. Both are reproduced manually inside the function: `user_id` is derived from `auth.uid()` server-side (a caller-supplied `user_id`/`contributed_by`/`creator_id`/`content_creator_id`/`is_verified` key anywhere in the card payload is rejected outright, not silently ignored), and any card whose `question_type` is in the D-10 verdict-bearing list (`mcq`, `mcq_multi`, `match_the_following`, `case_study_mcq`, `correct_incorrect`, `fitb`) is rejected unless `is_professor_or_admin()`. `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC, anon` then `GRANT EXECUTE ... TO authenticated` only — `anon` needed an explicit separate revoke (see hotfix below), `REVOKE ... FROM PUBLIC` alone did not cover it.
- **Two hotfixes surfaced by a live T1–T8 run, both grant-level, neither a logic bug** (`docs/database/sprint8.7/04_HOTFIX_grants.sql`): (1) the original `REVOKE ALL` on `flashcard_batch_provenance` stripped base table-level `SELECT` from `authenticated`, which the flashcards INSERT policy's `EXISTS(...)` subquery needs just to execute — without it, Postgres throws a hard "permission denied for table" instead of gracefully evaluating the RLS-filtered subquery to "0 rows → false" (the insert was still blocked either way, but via the wrong mechanism, which would also have permanently prevented the `EXISTS()` check from ever seeing a real match). Fixed by granting table-level `SELECT` to `authenticated` with **zero** RLS `SELECT` policy — RLS stays enabled with no policy, so `authenticated` still gets 0 rows back on any direct query; nothing becomes readable, the grant only unblocks the subquery mechanically. (2) `anon` could still execute `create_flashcard_batches` after `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC` — this project has a default-privileges rule that grants `EXECUTE` on new `public`-schema functions directly to `anon`/`authenticated`, not routed through `PUBLIC` (the same pattern Step 0's diagnostic already found on table-level grants). Fixed with an explicit `REVOKE ... FROM anon`.
- **Live T1–T8 verification (18/09/2026, all 18 checks PASS post-hotfix):** direct flashcard insert without provenance is RLS-rejected; direct writes to `flashcard_batch_provenance` (INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE) are all denied to `authenticated`; a note insert omitting provenance is trigger-rejected with the expected message; a legitimate owner editing a harmless field on a genuine pre-8.7.1 legacy note (NULL provenance) still succeeds, and its provenance stays NULL afterward (trigger is INSERT-only, confirmed); reusing an already-provenanced `batch_id` in a second RPC call is rejected rather than overwriting it; the RPC happy path creates exactly one provenance row + all cards for a 2-card batch, `created_by` correct; a multi-card call with one deliberately invalid card (missing `front_text`) rolls back atomically — zero provenance rows, zero cards survive; a student attempting `mcq` via the RPC is rejected with the verdict-type message, and a student attempting to inject another user's `user_id` into a card payload is rejected as malformed input — zero rows survive either attempt; `authenticated` can execute the RPC, `anon` and `PUBLIC` cannot.
- **Auditor sign-off (T1–T8 evidence, not inference):** moving flashcard creation behind `create_flashcard_batches()` has preserved every authorization guarantee direct-client RLS previously supplied (ownership via `auth.uid()`, the D-10 professor/admin verdict-type gate) and added a strictly new one (a batch cannot exist without a declared provenance source) — T7/T7b are the direct proof the RPC is not a privilege-escalation path.
- **Known bug, logged not fixed this sprint** (`docs/tracking/bugs.md`): `flashcards.source` defaults to `'manual'` for every insert, including bulk uploads — confirmed via code read, neither `FlashcardCreate.jsx` nor `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx` ever sets `source` explicitly, so bulk-uploaded cards are indistinguishable from manually-authored ones by `source` alone. Disposition deferred to Sprint 8.7.2's auditor.
- **⚠️ Breaking change, intentional:** once 01_SCHEMA was deployed, direct flashcard creation from `FlashcardCreate.jsx`/`BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx` — the only two live card-creation paths — started failing (RLS violation) for every user, since neither page creates a provenance row and neither calls the new RPC yet. This is expected and was confirmed before deployment: production flashcard creation is intentionally broken until Sprint 8.7.2/8.7.3 migrate those pages to `create_flashcard_batches()`.
- **Sprint 8.7.2 — service restoration + `source` bug closed (18/09/2026):** both live flashcard-creation paths (`FlashcardCreate.jsx` manual, `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx` CSV) migrated from the broken direct `flashcards` insert to `create_flashcard_batches()`. `notes` remain on the old path — out of scope, deferred to 8.7.3 (`NoteUpload.jsx`).
  - **RPC signature change:** `create_flashcard_batches(p_source_type, p_source_name, p_batches jsonb, p_creation_channel text)` — added `p_creation_channel` (`'manual' | 'bulk_upload' | 'gemini_import'`, validated server-side inside the function body since Postgres has no column-style `CHECK` for function parameters; `gemini_import` accepted but unused by any frontend path yet). Every row the RPC inserts now gets `flashcards.source = p_creation_channel` explicitly — closes the bug logged in 8.7.1 (below). Adding a parameter changes the function's identity arguments (Postgres overload identity is by parameter *types*, not names/defaults), so `CREATE OR REPLACE` on the 4-arg signature would have created a second overload alongside the 3-arg one rather than replacing it; the 3-arg overload was `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS`'d explicitly in the same migration (`docs/database/sprint8.7.2/01_FUNCTIONS_creation_channel.sql`) — safe because nothing in production could call the RPC successfully yet (that was the active outage). Live-verified post-deploy via a direct PostgREST call with the anon key: the new 4-arg signature resolves (`42501 permission denied for function` — reachable, correctly denied to `anon`), the old 3-arg call returns `PGRST202` (schema-cache miss — gone from the API surface, not just `pg_proc`).
  - **`scenario` column fix, found during Step 0 live diagnostic, not in the original 8.7.1 design:** the RPC's `INSERT INTO flashcards` column list omitted `scenario` entirely, even though it's a real, actively-used column (Sprint 7.10, `case_study_mcq`) both frontend paths set on every case row. Migrating either page onto the 8.7.1 RPC as deployed would have silently dropped every `case_study_mcq` card's scenario text. Fixed in the same migration (column added to both the INSERT list and the `SELECT ... FROM jsonb_array_elements`).
  - **`flashcards.deck_id` — resolved, not redesigned:** live diagnostic (500 most recent rows) showed 377/500 populated, 100% match rate against the 5-column-join resolution on a 100-row spot check. Confirmed as an active, correctly-maintained field for manually-created cards, not dead data — `StudyMode.jsx` and `MyFlashcards.jsx` both read it directly (`StudyMode.jsx` falls back to the 5-column join only when null). `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx` has never set it, before or after this sprint. Both frontend paths preserve `deck_id` in the RPC payload exactly as before — no schema or behavior change, just moved from a direct insert column to a field inside each card's JSON in `p_batches`.
  - **Frontend changes:** both pages gained a required "Content Source" section (`source_type` ∈ official_body/original_creator, `source_name`) applied once per save/upload; both replaced their direct `.from('flashcards').insert(...)` with `.rpc('create_flashcard_batches', ...)`, grouping their existing per-card `batch_id` values into the RPC's `{batch_id, cards[]}[]` shape without changing any batching/grouping logic; both strip `user_id`/`contributed_by`/`creator_id`/`content_creator_id`/`is_verified` from card payloads (server-derived, rejected outright by the RPC if present). D-10 rejection messages on both pages were changed to a generic "requires a professor/admin account" wording instead of enumerating restricted question types — the enumerated list would drift from `v_verdict_types` (the RPC's actual SSOT) silently; matched on the RPC's error message substring (`error.message.includes('professors/admins may author')`), not the Postgres error code (`P0001` is shared by every `RAISE EXCEPTION` in the function, so code alone can't distinguish D-10 from other validation failures).
  - **Live verification (18/09/2026, dev server → live Supabase, real student then professor session):** manual creation as a student (flashcard type) — succeeded, `source='manual'`, `is_verified=false`, `deck_id` populated, provenance row correct. D-10 rejection — tested directly against the deployed RPC (not just through the UI, since the UI already hides restricted types from students) via the student's own authenticated session: `mcq` attempt rejected with `P0001` / `"Only professors/admins may author verdict-bearing question_type \"mcq\""`, exactly matching the frontend's substring check. Atomicity — a direct multi-batch RPC call (one valid batch + one batch with a card missing `front_text`) was rejected and left zero rows in both `flashcards` and `flashcard_batch_provenance`, including from the individually-valid batch; confirmed this is the correct test to prove RPC-level atomicity because `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx`'s own CSV-parse validation (`parseCSV`) already rejects a row missing `front`/`back` before any RPC call is made, so a same-outcome test through the upload UI wouldn't have exercised the RPC's own atomicity guarantee at all. Professor-authorized `mcq` creation — succeeded, `source='manual'`, provenance `official_body` recorded correctly. Bulk CSV upload (1 plain card + a 2-question `case_study_mcq` case sharing one `case_group`) — one RPC call produced 2 distinct `batch_id`s (plain card + case group), `source='bulk_upload'` on all 3 rows (closing the bug), `scenario` text correctly present on both case rows, provenance recorded once per `batch_id` with matching `source_type`/`source_name`.
  - **`bugs.md` closed** the `flashcards.source` bug — live-verified `manual` → `'manual'` and `bulk_upload` → `'bulk_upload'`, not just from code inspection.
  - **Two more bugs caught by a `/code-review` pass before shipping, both fixed and live-verified, neither found by the manual test matrix above:**
    1. **`batch_description` silently dropped to NULL on every RPC-created card.** Both pages' batching step destructured `batch_description` off each card onto the outer `{batch_id, ...}` batch object — reads naturally as "one label per batch," but the RPC (both the 8.7.1 original and 8.7.2's revision) reads it per-card (`c->>'batch_description'`), never at the batch level. Fixed by leaving it on each card instead (`src/lib/flashcardBatches.js`'s `groupCardsIntoBatches` after the extraction below). Live-verified via a real bulk upload with a batch label: `batch_description` now correctly populated.
    2. **Bulk-upload verified badge silently lost.** Pre-migration, `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx` set `is_verified: isProfessor || isAdmin || isSuperAdmin`; the RPC hardcoded `is_verified = false` for every row with no role branch. Since the RPC had zero live callers before this sprint's frontend migration, the hardcoded `false` reached production for the first time only once the migration shipped — a real regression this migration activated, not one the review invented. Fixed via `docs/database/sprint8.7.2/08_HOTFIX_bulk_verified_badge.sql`: `is_verified = (p_creation_channel = 'bulk_upload' AND v_is_prof_admin)`, still fully server-derived (never caller-supplied — does not reopen the privilege-escalation concern the function's own SECURITY-CRITICAL comment warns about). Manual creation (`p_creation_channel = 'manual'`) unaffected — it never set `is_verified = true` even pre-8.7.1. Live-verified: a professor bulk upload now shows `is_verified = true`; a professor manual `mcq` creation still shows `is_verified = false`, matching prior behavior for each path exactly.
  - **Post-review refactor (user-requested, same session):** the batching-Map logic and the D-10 error-substring check, which had been duplicated near-identically across both pages, were extracted into `src/lib/flashcardBatches.js` (`groupCardsIntoBatches`, `isD10Rejection`, `D10_REJECTION_MESSAGE`) — the same shared-`src/lib`-module pattern this project already uses for `caseStudyMcq.js`/`mcq.js`/`fitb.js` between these two files. The "Content Source" form section was extracted into `src/components/flashcards/ContentSourceFields.jsx` (a `compact` prop preserves `BulkUploadFlashcards.jsx`'s denser stepper styling vs. `FlashcardCreate.jsx`'s default Card spacing — pure visual parity, confirmed via before/after screenshots, not a behavior change). Re-verified live post-refactor: manual creation and bulk upload both still succeed through the shared code path.
  - **Status: manual + bulk flashcard creation service RESTORED, live-verified, post-review-fixes applied.** `notes` (`NoteUpload.jsx`) remain on the pre-8.7.1 path and unaffected by the provenance gate (separate mechanism) — deferred to 8.7.3 regardless. 8.7.4 still owns the provenance read policy/UI display work deliberately deferred in 8.7.1.
- **Sprint 8.7.3 — notes creation path restored, D-21 closed (18/09/2026):** the last unmigrated write path. Much smaller scope than 8.7.2 — the notes provenance mechanism (`content_source_type`/`content_source_name` columns + `trg_require_note_provenance` BEFORE INSERT trigger) already shipped fully-formed in 8.7.1; notes have no batching concept, so there is no RPC, no atomicity concern, and no `create_flashcard_batches`-style redesign here — this sprint is UI wiring against an enforcement mechanism that was already live and already tested (8.7.1 T3/T4).
  - **Step 0 live diagnostic, captured via a direct PostgREST call (anon key + the session's own access token, bypassing supabase-js since it isn't exposed as a page global) from an authenticated student session:** confirmed the trigger is still live and blocking a bare note insert — `status 400`, `{"code":"P0001","details":null,"hint":null,"message":"content_source_type and content_source_name are required on note creation"}`. Also confirmed `notes.content_creator_id` does not exist (`42703 column notes.content_creator_id does not exist`) — no field-stripping concern analogous to 8.7.2's `user_id`/`contributed_by`/etc. stripping, since notes carry no such caller-suppliable ownership fields in the first place.
  - **Frontend change, `NoteUpload.jsx` only:** added the same `ContentSourceFields.jsx` component 8.7.2 built for the flashcard pages, reused as-is (its single `{sourceType, sourceName}` interface already fits a single-item form — no fork needed since notes have exactly one provenance declaration per note, not one per batch). Added client-side required checks (`sourceType`/`sourceName.trim()`) matching `FlashcardCreate.jsx`'s pattern, and added `content_source_type`/`content_source_name` to the existing direct `.insert()` payload — no RPC, the trigger is the sole enforcement layer.
