Mapped the trigger's `P0001` rejection (matched by `error.code === 'P0001'` plus a message substring, the same code+substring pairing 8.7.2 used for D-10 since `P0001` is shared by every `RAISE EXCEPTION` in this codebase) to an actionable toast instead of the raw Postgres message.
  - **`NoteEdit.jsx` untouched, confirmed not just assumed:** provenance is set at creation only, per the sprint's own non-goal — no versioning, no edit-time provenance field. Live-verified via the real UI (8.7.1 only proved this in raw SQL) by opening a genuine pre-8.7 note with NULL provenance (`"Seed Note for Screenshots"`, TestOutlook's own), editing its description, and saving: succeeded, `content_source_type`/`content_source_name` stayed NULL afterward, description reverted post-verification.
  - **Live verification (18/09/2026, local dev → live Supabase, real TestOutlook student session; file-input steps done via a synthetic `File`/`DataTransfer` injection — the Browser pane has no native file-picker automation, same limitation noted in Sprint 8.6b/8.6c):** (1) ordinary note upload with `original_creator` source selected — succeeded, `content_source_type`/`content_source_name` correct on the row (verified via direct REST read, not just the success toast). (2) Group-sharing regression check, specifically flagged in the sprint brief since it's the line immediately after the changed insert call: uploaded a second note with `visibility='study_groups'` and one group selected — succeeded, `"Note uploaded and shared with 1 group(s)"`, and a `content_group_shares` row was confirmed to exist for the correct `content_id`/`group_id`. Both verification notes and their storage objects deleted post-verification, the group-share row deleted, confirmed via direct REST re-reads. (3) No-source-selected path is blocked client-side by the required-field checks before any request is sent; the DB-level backstop was verified directly in Step 0 rather than by attempting a UI bypass, since the observed `P0001`/message shape there is exactly what the new catch-block mapping matches against.
  - **No new SQL this sprint** — confirmed in Step 0, not assumed: the trigger and columns were already live since 8.7.1, so this is a frontend-only deploy.
  - **Status: creation-side D-21 shipped** (8.7.2/8.7.3). Display-side (8.7.4) below.
- **Sprint 8.7.4 — provenance display, ⚠️ CODE COMPLETE, NOT YET DEPLOYED, NOT LIVE-VERIFIED (18/09/2026):** builds the read policy + UI display 8.7.1 deliberately deferred. Full detail in now.md — summarized here.
  - **Read policy:** `authenticated_read_flashcard_batch_provenance`, unconditional `FOR SELECT TO authenticated USING (true)` on `flashcard_batch_provenance` — not a mirror of flashcards' own private/friends/public visibility. Provenance (source type + name) isn't sensitive per-batch content the way flashcard text is; mirroring full visibility logic here is real complexity for no privacy gain, and a place to re-litigate D-04. Flagged as a judgment call in the sprint brief itself, not a technical constraint — recorded, not yet independently reviewed against a live run.
  - **Display, flashcards (batch-level):** `MyFlashcards.jsx` (batch-group header), `StudyMode.jsx` (both entry paths — direct deck click and via `ReviewSession.jsx`'s `get_study_queue`), `NoteDetail.jsx` (linked flashcards list), `ReviewFlashcards.jsx`/Browse Study Sets (deck tile, server-aggregated). Shared `src/components/content/ProvenanceBadge.jsx` + `src/lib/provenance.js`.
  - **Display, notes (row-level, no ambiguity):** `MyNotes.jsx`, `NoteDetail.jsx` (the note itself — `SELECT *` already had the columns, only the render was added), `BrowseNotes.jsx` (via `get_browsable_notes` v4).
  - **Deck-level provenance is inherently ambiguous — design call made and flagged, not silently resolved:** a deck (5-grouping-column bucket) routinely spans many `batch_id`s. `get_browsable_decks` v7 only returns a non-null `provenance_source_type`/`_name` when every visible card in the deck shares exactly one `batch_id` with a provenance row; any multi-batch or legacy deck gets `NULL` in both columns — same "absence = render nothing" rule used everywhere else in D-21, not an invented "mixed sources" treatment.
  - **`get_study_queue` gained `batch_id`** (found via Step 0, not assumed — the live function had no `batch_id` at all, which would have left cards reached via the normal "Review due cards" flow — the most common entry point — permanently unbadged even with provenance data present). **Compatibility note, added on auditor request:** `get_study_queue`'s return shape carries no "frozen" or "locked" designation anywhere in this doc or `context.md` — the only locked-design-choices section in this codebase (`context.md` "Landing Page Design Decisions") is scoped to `Home.jsx`'s wordmark/CTA/stat-labels, unrelated. There IS direct precedent for additive changes to this exact function: the SRS Ladder Epic (§ above, "Changed: `get_study_queue` gains `rung` in its return shape") already extended it once, via the identical `DROP FUNCTION` + `CREATE` + re-applied-grants pattern this sprint reuses, with the same compatibility reasoning — additive trailing column, no existing caller reads it, so no existing caller's behavior changes. This sprint follows that established precedent rather than setting a new one.
  - **Consumer-side gap found and fixed by the auditor's own review request:** adding the column to the RPC does not automatically reach every consumer. `ReviewSession.jsx` (the actual "Today's Reviews" due-queue entry point) maps `get_study_queue` rows into a plain object literal (`cleanedCards`) before passing them to `StudyMode.jsx` — the initial 8.7.4 change added `batch_id` to the RPC but never added `batch_id: row.batch_id` to this mapping, so the RPC-level fix was silently incomplete on the client: cards reached via "Today's Reviews" would still never show a badge, only cards reached via `StudyMode.jsx`'s own direct deck-browse fetch (which does `SELECT *` and had `batch_id` all along) would. This was caught by re-tracing every RPC consumer during the auditor's requested reconciliation, not by the original live verification, which happened to only exercise the direct-fetch path. Fixed (one line, `src/pages/dashboard/Study/ReviewSession.jsx`).
  - **Incidental fix, found while reproducing `get_browsable_notes` for its DROP+CREATE:** v3 had no `SET search_path` clause at all, unlike every other `SECURITY DEFINER` function in this codebase (L3 `17c` outage lesson, §1.11) — v4 pins it.
  - **Scope decision:** `get_public_deck_preview`/`get_public_note_preview` (the two anonymous-reachable preview RPCs) deliberately left untouched — no provenance columns, no display. Satisfies the brief's own regression concern (don't break anonymous visitors) trivially, since neither file is touched at all.
  - **✅ SQL deployed and live-verified by the operator (this session had no Supabase SQL access of its own — every file was written here, run by the operator, results reconciled turn-by-turn):**
  - Step 0 diagnostic reconciled clean: zero SELECT policies pre-existed (matched), all three RPC signatures matched the `DROP FUNCTION` statements exactly, `notes.content_source_type`/`content_source_name` confirmed nullable text, 7 live provenance rows already existed pre-deploy.
  - **Real bug found live, not caught by review: `get_browsable_decks` v7's first deploy was broken.** `min(fc.batch_id)` doesn't compile against `uuid` at runtime — Postgres has no default ordering operator class for uuid (`42883: function min(uuid) does not exist`). The DROP+CREATE itself succeeded, so the function existed but **every call failed** until the operator's live T4 run surfaced it. Fixed to `(array_agg(fc.batch_id))[1]` (safe because the surrounding `CASE` already gates on `count(DISTINCT fc.batch_id) = 1`), re-deployed, re-verified.
  - **T1 confirmed (real result row); T4/T5/T6 confirmed against real rows** (not NOTICE output): `get_browsable_decks` correctly resolves provenance for exactly the one single-batch deck in a 20-row sample and correctly returns NULL for the other 19 (legacy/multi-batch); `get_browsable_notes` returns the new columns (all NULL in-sample, expected — no notes created since 8.7.3 shipped today); `get_study_queue`'s `batch_id` spot-checked exactly against `flashcards.batch_id` for a real row. **T2/T3/T7 — correction, not independently confirmed:** these are `RAISE NOTICE` checks; the operator's run didn't error, but the actual NOTICE text was never captured back to this session, so "PASS" for these three was an inference from silence, not verified evidence. Flagged explicitly rather than left as an overclaim.
  - **§3 reconciliation pass — ✅ COMPLETE, live-verified via a real TestOutlook student session (dev server → live Supabase, 18/09/2026):** one manual flashcard, one bulk upload (2 cards, synthetic `File`/`DataTransfer` injection), and one note (synthetic image injection) created in sequence — no cross-path regressions, provenance badges confirmed rendering correctly on every surface (`MyFlashcards.jsx`, `StudyMode.jsx` per-card, `MyNotes.jsx`, `NoteDetail.jsx`). This also produced a genuine live multi-batch deck (the new cards + the pre-existing 8.7.2 test card all landed in the same subject/topic), confirming `get_browsable_decks`' "unambiguous single-batch only" badge rule holds against real data, not just the diagnostic sample — Browse Study Sets correctly shows no badge on that deck. T7-equivalent re-confirmed via a direct PostgREST call to `create_flashcard_batches` with `question_type: 'mcq'` as the student — rejected with the identical `P0001` message as pre-8.7.4, confirming this sprint's read-only RPC changes left the D-10 creation-path gate untouched. §3(c) satisfied by the scope decision above (public preview RPCs untouched).
  - **D-21 status: FULLY re-confirmed accurate post-8.7.4, live-verified end to end.** Test data cleanup run and re-verified: `remaining_flashcards = 0`, `remaining_notes = 0`, `remaining_provenance = 0`, all three confirmed by the operator, not inferred (`docs/database/sprint8.7.4/06_CLEANUP_remove_verification_test_data.sql`) — the Browser pane's sandbox suppresses the native `confirm()` dialog the in-app delete buttons need, same limitation sprint8.6c hit, so cleanup went through SQL instead of the UI.

| Task | Why | Notes |
|------|-----|-------|
| **PostHog integration** | Listed in Privacy Policy; not in code | Install `posthog-js`; init in `src/main.jsx`; API key from posthog.com |
| **Sentry integration** | Listed in Privacy Policy; not in code | Install `@sentry/react`; init in `src/main.jsx`; DSN from sentry.io |
| **Remove `tesseract.js`** | Dead dependency | Verify no imports; remove from package.json |
| **Delete `MigrateNoteImages.jsx`** | ⚠️ NOT yet complete — verified via SQL 16/09/2026: 12 live notes still reference images above the 400KB threshold. Do not delete until a re-run of that check returns 0 rows. | Also remove its route from App.jsx once confirmed |
| **Replace native `confirm()`/`prompt()` with accessible app dialogs for role-change + Hard Delete, then screenshot those** | Deferred, not waived, from Sprint 8.3's screenshot plan per Quality Auditor review (16/09/2026). `SuperAdminDashboard.jsx`'s role-change and Hard Delete flows both use plain native browser dialogs — poor UX for two high-consequence/destructive actions in their own right, and not something worth checking a screenshot of into product documentation (generic OS/browser chrome, renders differently per browser). Build real, accessible, in-app confirmation dialogs for both (styled, keyboard-accessible, consistent with the rest of the admin UI), then capture the 2 remaining help screenshots against the new dialogs. | Not just a screenshot task — a real UX fix first. Until this ships, `admin-batch-groups`'s screenshot set stays at 3 of 5 by design, not by oversight. |
| **Help & Guide: more hyperlinks** | Sprint 8.3 applied the new `[label](/route)` syntax only to the Sprint 8.2 batch-lifecycle sections as a proof of the feature. Other sections still have plain-text "Go to X" mentions that could be linked. | Optional polish, not a defect. |
| **Pull live `create_study_group`/`get_user_groups` definitions via `pg_get_functiondef`** | Sprint 8.4's Step 0 audit found both have live bodies not present anywhere in the repo's SQL docs — `CreateGroup.jsx`/`MyGroups.jsx` call parameters/columns absent from every filed version. Blocks any future `group_type`/`is_batch_group` schema collapse until resolved. | Introspection only, no behavior change by itself. |
| **Revision-phase indicator for the Exam Runway state** | Proposed during Sprint 8.4's audit review (16/09/2026) as a stronger early-warning layer than a bare days-count: derive a named phase from days-until-month-begins (e.g. "Build & Strengthen" → "Intensive Revision" → "Final Consolidation" as the runway shortens), shown alongside `EXAM RUNWAY · {Month Year}` / `{days} days until {Month} begins`. Creates urgency through a changing preparation phase, not just a shrinking number — answers "what should this period feel like," not only "how long is left." | Needs real pedagogical judgment (phase boundaries, per-phase guidance copy) — a design decision, not an inline implementation one. Deliberately not built this sprint. |
| **Per-category study-time breakdown** | D-18 scoped categorization at logging time only, explicitly not a new report. A breakdown by category (`Progress.jsx`, professor analytics) is a real, valuable follow-up once enough categorized data exists. | Not built. `study_sessions.category` is additive metadata only this sprint — no read path consumes it yet. |
| **First-pass vs. revision flag on study-log categories** | D-18 dropped a "Revision/Recap" category because it mixed a purpose/timing axis into an activity-type axis. If that signal is wanted, it belongs as an orthogonal flag on any of the 5 existing categories, not a 6th category. | Not built — no brief for it yet. |
| **`sprint7.3/03_TEST_verify_study_time_and_goal_prompt.sql` likely has the same `auth.uid()` NULL bug Sprint 8.5's `02_TEST` hit** | Found while fixing Sprint 8.5's own test file (16/09/2026): `auth.uid()` resolves to `NULL` in the Supabase SQL Editor's no-session context, so any test SQL relying on it for a real write/read scoped to "the calling user" either errors or silently returns nothing useful if re-run there. `sprint7.3/03_TEST` uses `auth.uid()` the same way. Not verified whether it was originally run some other way (with a session) — not re-run to check. | Test-harness debt, not a product bug. Non-blocking; fix opportunistically if that file is ever re-run. |

### 3.3 Infrastructure Reference

All of the below lives in Supabase Dashboard / Vercel — NOT in code. Update here when changed.

**Email / SMTP**
- Provider: Google Workspace via Supabase SMTP Settings
- From: `hello@revisop.com` | Sender name: RevisOp
- SMTP host: smtp.gmail.com, port 587
- Auth: Google Workspace App Password for `hello@revisop.com` (requires 2-Step Verification ON)
- DNS: SPF, DKIM, DMARC all configured for revisop.com. Email may land in spam during initial reputation period (new domain — reputation builds over weeks).
- Recovery phone: 098207 61284; recovery email: anand@mtcpl.net (Google account recovery for hello@revisop.com)

**Supabase Auth Settings**
- Email confirmation: ON
- Site URL: `https://www.revisop.com`
- Redirect URL whitelist: `https://www.revisop.com/**`
- Password reset email redirect must point to `https://www.revisop.com` (not `recallapp.co.in` or localhost)

**Vercel Domains**
- Primary: `www.revisop.com`
- Old domains redirect to primary: `www.recallapp.co.in`, `recallapp.co.in`, `revisop.com` (apex)

**Supabase Realtime**
- `notifications` table: Realtime replication enabled (Dashboard → Database → Replication)
- If bell icon stops updating live: check this toggle first

**Supabase Secrets**
- `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`, `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`, `VAPID_SUBJECT` (= mailto:hello@revisop.com)
- `CRON_SECRET` — shared by all pg_cron-triggered Edge Functions; if rotated, ALL cron jobs must be resynced immediately or they return 401
- `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`

**pg_cron Jobs**
- `daily-review-reminders`: `30 2 * * *` (02:30 UTC daily)
- `cron-daily-study-summary`: `*/15 * * * *` (every 15 min)

**Environment Variables (Vercel)**
- `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`
- `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`
- `VITE_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`

### 3.4 Brand Identity

**Colors**
- Amber: `#f59e0b` (Tailwind `amber-500`) — primary brand, CTAs, highlights
- Navy: `#1e1b4b` (Tailwind `indigo-950`) — dark text, backgrounds, nav
- Background: `bg-background` (shadcn CSS variable = white/#ffffff) — all auth and public pages

**Wordmark Pattern** (used everywhere a logo appears)
```jsx
<span className="text-2xl font-bold tracking-tight leading-none">
  <span style={{ color: '#f59e0b' }}>Revis</span>
  <span style={{ color: '#1e1b4b' }}>Op</span>
</span>
```

**PWA / Browser**
- `index.html` theme-color: `#1e1b4b` (controls Android browser status bar)
- `public/site.webmanifest` theme_color: `#1e1b4b` (controls installed PWA status bar)
- `short_name`: RevisOp | `name`: RevisOp — CA Study App
- PWA icons: `/android-chrome-192x192.png`, `/android-chrome-512x512.png` (amber + navy)
- Apple touch icon: `/apple-touch-icon.png`
- To see updated PWA icon: uninstall old PWA → reinstall from `www.revisop.com`

**Color Mapping from Rebrand** (for any future reference)
- `bg-blue-*/indigo-*/purple-*` → `bg-amber-*` or `bg-[#1e1b4b]`
- `text-blue-*/indigo-*/purple-*` → `text-amber-*` or `text-[#1e1b4b]`
- Gradient backgrounds → flat `bg-amber-50` or `bg-background`
- Dark buttons → `bg-[#1e1b4b]`
- Recharts bar fill `#6366f1` → `#f59e0b`
- Heatmap blue scale → amber/navy scale
